# Defect register

One list of every known defect: what is open, and what was fixed, when and
where. Started on 2026-09-18, because defects were being recorded in three
places -- `docs/BACKLOG.md` §31, the "Known defects" notes at the end of each
section of `docs/INDEPENDENT_TEST_CASES.md`, and PR descriptions -- and nobody
could answer "is there anything open?" without reading all three.

**This file is the index; the detail stays where it was found.** Each row
links to the full write-up. Status here was checked against the code on
2026-09-18 unless the row says otherwise.

## How to use it

- **Found a defect:** add a row to **Open** with the next id for its area
  (`D-BUY-9`, `D-SELL-1`, ...), the date, a one-line summary, the evidence, and
  a link to where it is written up. Keep an existing id when it already has one
  (`D-27-4`, `BL-31.13`).
- **Fixed it:** move the row to **Fixed**, add the date, the PR, and the test
  that fails when the fix is reverted. A fix without such a test is not done.
- **Decided it is not a defect:** move it to **Not a defect** with the reason.
- **Severity.** *High* -- wrong money, stock or tax, or a rule that can be
  bypassed. *Medium* -- a wrong record or a broken screen with a workaround.
  *Low* -- wording, a missing label, a convenience.
- **Evidence.** *Live* -- seen in real rows or on screen. *Code* -- read off the
  code and not yet driven. Drive a *Code* row before fixing it.

---

### Buying -- found writing the Buying data trail, 2026-09-18

Full write-ups: PR #435 and `docs/DATA_TRAIL_BY_OPERATION.md` §9.

| Id | Severity | Summary | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-PRC-94 | Low | **A supplier bill raised off an order line that charges nothing (a line with only free goods), with the receipt stage off, is refused in the ledger's words** ("A journal entry needs at least one debit and one credit line."), and the free goods are not brought in. Raised once through an order of 0 PIECE at 60.00 with 5 free, and once with 1 PIECE at 0.00. With the free-only line beside a charged line the first bill brings in all 5. Workaround: put the free-only line on an order with a charged line, or raise a goods receipt where the stage is on. | Live, one firm, reproduced twice: PRCQ-94 in `docs/qa/PRICING_API_CHECK_ROUND_10_2026-10-07.md`; not looked up in code |
| D-BUY-68 | Low | **Purchase returns slice the header discount at four places**, so a paisa can differ from the bill's. | Reported as left over by the agent that fixed D-PRC-93 (#1321); not traced further |
| D-BUY-69 | Low | **Record Payment takes the value of a purchase return that spilled onto a second bill off the named bill only.** The units come off two bills, the money off one. | Reported as left over by the agents that fixed D-PRC-73 and D-PRC-83 (#1306, #1317); the totals agree |
| D-BUY-70 | Low | **A purchase return whose completion is refused because another return overtook it says "debit note approved since", and cannot say which document changed.** | Reported as left over by the agents that fixed D-PRC-73 and D-PRC-81 (#1306, #1315) |
| D-BUY-72 | Low | **A draft purchase return raised off a bill takes the room of a debit note**, which is then refused as "1440.00 already returned" when nothing has gone back. The wording is not true of a draft. | Live: round 7 gaps in `docs/qa/PRICING_API_CHECK_ROUND_7_2026-10-06.md` (R1, S3) |
| D-BUY-71 | Low | **A supplier's bill can be paid in full while a credit against it stands**: the payment is taken and the supplier then owes the firm 849.60 (R1, S3). The books agree, and the payables report nets the credit (#1317). | Live: round 7 gaps in `docs/qa/PRICING_API_CHECK_ROUND_7_2026-10-06.md` |
| D-BUY-65 | Low | **A store that issued self-invoices as `SI-` or rate contracts as `RC-` before migration `20261005_0328` can still have a new sales invoice or receipt take one of those old numbers.** The migration moves the rule to `RSI-` / `RTC-` and leaves issued numbers alone, and a number is checked against documents of its own type and journal references only. No store that started after the migration is affected. Left as a known limit: renumbering issued tax documents is worse than the clash. | Live: BUYQ-22 in `docs/qa/PURCHASING_API_CHECK_ROUND_3_2026-10-05.md` (three clashes made on two round 2 firms) |

### Compliance -- found writing the Compliance data trail, 2026-09-19

Full write-ups: the PR that added `docs/DATA_TRAIL_BY_OPERATION.md` §13, and
the section itself. Live rows are a `compliance-firm` store built for the pass
and driven against the running backend (`fx_t0919l8ca_g`), the two
`compliance-firm` stores of 2026-09-16 (`fx_t0916irn8_g`, `fx_t09167ru4_g`),
the selling stores, WHOLE01, the shared store and ELEC01, read with SELECT
only. Checked and clean: no registration or e-way bill in any store is LIVE or
unmarked, and `mode` is NOT NULL with no default in every store checked;
`portal_for("LIVE")` raises; `simulate` never commits (the endpoint does);
rules are evaluated `priority`, `code`, `version_number` desc on NOT NULL
columns and evaluation stops at the first match (the trace of a matched run
lists four of six rules); the profile resolver ranks NULL `effective_from`
explicitly; no module reads the local clock; TCS is charged on the receipt,
only on the excess over the threshold, summed from receipts per the firm's own
year, posted to 2500 (WHOLE01's 2500 equals its collections, 648.24); every
e-invoice, GSTR and TCS read is scoped to the firm in context; GSTR-3B is summed
from the documents, not from GSTR-1.

| Id | Severity | Summary | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-CMP-24 | High | **Input credit is not reversed on stock given away free, as samples, lost or written off** (s.17(5)(h) of the CGST Act: goods given as gifts or free samples, and goods lost, stolen, destroyed or written off). Stock adjustments with the reasons FREE_TO_CUSTOMER and SAMPLE (`app/inventory/services/adjustment_reasons.py`; `inventory_service.py` maps SAMPLE to promotional expense) and write-offs post the cost only. `itc_reversals` is written only by `app/gst_returns/services/rule37.py`, which reverses unpaid bills. **Held for the owner's chartered accountant**: which reasons reverse. To be built in the inventory and compliance passes, with the reversal reported in GSTR-3B 4(B)(1) and the reasons configurable per firm. | Code: market study `docs/qa/PRICING_MARKET_GAPS_2026-10-06.md`, finding read again in the code on 2026-10-06; not driven |
| D-CMP-25 | Low | **`/gst-returns/checks` IRN_MISSING ignores sales returns**, though a return that credits a bill needs an IRN (D-PRC-89). | Reported as left over by the agent that fixed D-PRC-89 (#1318) |
| D-CMP-26 | Low | **A return of BOX off a bill typed in PIECE can make an HSN row for BOX negative.** The unit is the firm's own code; there is no UQC mapping. | Reported as left over by the agent that fixed D-PRC-50 and D-PRC-51 (#1284, #1286) |
| D-CMP-27 | Low | **`reporting_window.py` asks `link.in_(...)` with an unchunked list**, which breaks past 65,535 ids (`over_chunks`, `app/core/utils/chunks.py`). | Code: reported by the agent that fixed D-PRC-89 (#1318); not driven at volume |

Found in the pricing pass, ten rounds over HTTP on 2026-10-06 and 07 (`docs/qa/PRICING_API_CHECK_ROUND_1_2026-10-06.md` to `..._ROUND_10_2026-10-07.md`); the D-PRC rows above are the ones left, and the rest are under Fixed.

### Identity, firms and audit -- found writing the data trail, 2026-09-19

Full write-ups: the PR that added `docs/DATA_TRAIL_BY_OPERATION.md` §15, and
the section itself. Live rows are `platform`, every store's `audit_logs`
trigger (58 schemas in `agency_platform` and `electrolink_ops`), and three
fixture runs driven against the running backend -- `platform-operator`
`t0919tkcy`, `platform-admin` `t0919wwzu`, `two-firm-user` `t0919subq` -- plus
a probe firm `IDNPROBE1`, created and deleted unprovisioned. **D-IDN-1 and
D-IDN-2 are privilege escalations and were deliberately not driven**; the
evidence is the code path. Checked and clean: no firm-owned service reads
`users`, `firms` or `user_firms` on a tenant session (delivery note, purchase,
sales order, territory and print support all go through `platform_reader()`
or the platform session); every firm route and readiness open the named
firm's store with `firm_store_session`; no private firm-scope resolver on a
firm-owned route; every enforced code is seeded; the reserved role codes are
refused case-folded and `RoleUpdate` cannot rename a code; email, firm code,
GST, PAN and the active primary are unique among live rows by partial index
as well as by the service; no two firms share a database/schema pair; routing
is refused on update; a deactivated, expired or deleted user is refused on
the next request and the next refresh, and holds no live refresh token;
every change that alters a token's grants bumps `authorization_version`; no
module in scope reads the local clock; `/me/firms` ranks with an explicit
`case`, not NULL order; the append-only trigger and its function are in every
store; retention is opt-in by design and has not run here.

| Id | Severity | Summary | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

D-IDN-12 is under Fixed (2026-10-05).

### Territory, commission and targets -- found writing the data trail, 2026-09-19

Full write-ups: the PR that added `docs/DATA_TRAIL_BY_OPERATION.md` §17, and the
section itself. Live rows are one `commission-firm` fixture run driven against
the running backend, `t0919tkvs` (firm `T0919TKVS-T`, schema `fx_t0919tkvs_t`),
plus every store on the local server read with SELECT only (59 schemas in
`agency_platform` and `electrolink_ops`); nothing was written to the demo firms.
Checked and clean: a payout is snapshotted at accrual and nothing downstream
re-reads the report; `UQ_commission_payouts_period_active` is partial in both
dialects and the overlap read stands beside it; the accrual, the payment and the
reversal carry three distinct references (`…`, `-PAY`, `-REV`) and the reversal
mirrors the accrual exactly; a payout's `status` is refused on its update body
(422) and the commission rule and payout updates are partial
(`exclude_unset`, `slabs` guarded on omission); a rule with slabs ignores its
flat rate in `_earned`, and the desktop labels the box "Rate (unused)" and
shows no single rate for a ladder; a margin line with a NULL cost contributes
nothing; the governing rule is resolved per row on its own date and each rule's
subtotal is laddered separately; the Unassigned bucket earns nothing and is
never paid; a round's reorder releases only the stop numbers actually moving and
flushes before writing them; a blank route is judged on the document's own
date (an order dated after the window closed kept its territory and took no
route); the four bulk routes and the territory import stage and commit once;
customers on a round and outlets on a plan are checked for the firm; a
salesperson put on a round, or named on an order, note or invoice, is checked
for membership through the platform store; every name is read through
`FirmMetadataReader` or `platform_reader()`; nothing reads the local clock;
the call list ranks unplaced stops on an explicit `case`; every list declares
its page bounds; every audit row these modules write lands in the firm's own
store with its firm. Commission was measured on the document total, tax and
freight included; decided by Claude, industry standard, on 2026-09-24 that it
is earned on net sales -- tax and freight earn nothing (PR #644,
`docs/COMMISSION_FRAMEWORK.md`).

| Id | Severity | Summary | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

None open (2026-10-01).

### Reports, the dashboard, global search and diagnostics -- found writing the data trail, 2026-09-23

Full write-ups: the PR that added `docs/DATA_TRAIL_BY_OPERATION.md` §18, and the
section itself. Live rows are six fixture runs on TEST01 driven against the
running backend -- `accountant` `t0923zemi`, `firm-admin` `t0923gcby`,
`po-invoiced` `t0923vm0u`, `po-received` `t09233p7o`, `po-approved`
`t092343ic`, `invoiced` `t092359jg` -- plus WHOLE01, MEDI01, FOOD01, the shared
store and `platform` read with SELECT only or searched as a platform
administrator; nothing was written to the demo firms. Checked and clean: no
report, search or probe writes a row; every report reads `utc_now()` and never
the clock; every header query carries `firm_id`; the sales-order reports and
the search read `users` through `platform_reader()` and nothing reads a
platform table on the tenant session; the search checks membership of the firm
named before any query, and reads `users`, `roles`, `permissions` and `firms`
on the platform store; the loyalty balance is a sum over the ledger and its
worth is walked batch by batch; the promotion reports key an offer by
`version_group_id` and count a claim only at CLAIMED; the diagnostics ingest
takes firm and user from the caller, caps every field, and the server-side
write swallows its own failure; the dashboard is platform-only by designation
and hides each count the caller lacks the code for; every list here declares
its page bounds. D-RPT-2 and D-RPT-3 are the same defect on either side of the
ledger and D-RPT-10 to 12 the same one in three modules; each is one row
because each is one fix.

**D-RPT-1 to 17 and D-RPT-19 are fixed** (#585 to #600 on 2026-09-23, #613 and
#616 on 2026-09-24; rows under Fixed). Four fixes
rest on a product decision taken as industry standard, recorded on the row and
open to the owner: `REPORT_VIEW` opens every report (D-RPT-4); an unapproved
draft owes no stock (D-RPT-7); a back order is an oldest-first allocation of
today's on-hand stock (D-RPT-8); a proforma is settled when its order is paid
or invoiced (D-RPT-11).

| Id | Severity | Summary | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-PERF-3 | Low | **A large response is reset in transit on the development PC.** `GET /finance/journal-entries?page_size=100` failed 8 of 8 tries (WinError 10054 after about 19 seconds) through two HTTP clients and `GET /customers?page_size=100` (122,673 bytes) 4 of 8, while the server logged 200 in under 100 ms; `page_size=25` always worked. Seen with 1.8 GB of memory free and another process provisioning firms. Recurred in round 5 on one route, `GET /inventory/ledger` with `page_size=100` (about 175 KB, 14 resets in four runs). On an idle machine `GET /openapi.json` (4.3 MB) was fetched 8 times out of 8, so size alone is not it. **Narrowed on 2026-10-09 (inventory round 4): it is neither memory nor the reloader, and it is repeatable.** A request that carries `Connection: close` loses any answer longer than 327,680 bytes: the client reads exactly that much, waits 19 seconds and is reset, every time, while the same request without the header reads the whole answer in 0.2 seconds. Python's `urllib` sends that header on every request, which is why the check scripts saw it and the desktop, which keeps its connections alive, never did; `GET /inventory/summary/by-product` (392,721 bytes on the fixture firm) failed 6 of 6 through it. It is not this application's code: a 20-line Starlette app returning 400,000 bytes does the same on this PC (uvicorn 0.51.0, Python 3.14.6, Windows), with either HTTP parser (`--http h11` and httptools), while a bare ASGI app that sends the same bytes does not. The check client now keeps its connection (`fetch` in `docs/qa/checks/common.py`), so the kept checks no longer meet it. Still open because a customer's client can: a reverse proxy that talks HTTP/1.0 to the server (nginx's default), or any script built on `urllib`, would lose every large list. Next step, attended: try the server library's next release and the other event loop on a Windows PC, and put the 20-line case in the integration suite. | Live, one firm, one evening: SELLQ-32 in `docs/qa/SELLING_API_CHECK_ROUND_4_2026-10-05.md`; `docs/qa/INVENTORY_API_CHECK_ROUND_4_2026-10-09.md` |

### Screen tests -- found clicking the phase 2 screens against the running server, 2026-10-07

Cases are in `docs/qa/SCREEN_TEST_CASES_BUY_SELL_PRICE.md`; results per case id in the `SCREEN_FLOW_CHECK_ROUND_*` files beside it. D-UI-13, 14, 16, 17, 19 to 25, 27 to 30, 32 to 34, 37, 40 to 42, 44, 46, 48, 50 to 55 and 57 to 59 are under Fixed; D-UI-15, 18, 35, 36, 43, 45 and 47 under Not a defect. A register id is the finding's `SCRQ` number in the check files up to 59 (no finding was numbered 49, so there is no D-UI-49); D-UI-60 to 63 were left over by the fixes and have no `SCRQ` number; D-UI-64 to 71 are round 2e's (quotations, proforma), numbered as their `SCRQ` findings, and were fixed together on 2026-10-08 (under Fixed).

| Id | Found | Summary | Severity | Evidence | Where |

## Fixed

| Id | Fixed | Summary | PR | Guard |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-STK-41 | 2026-10-08 | **A stock movement that posts no journal took any date** (Low; the owner's rule of 2026-10-08): a transfer, a quarantine hold or release, a transfer document (saved, changed, dispatched or received), a stock count (drawn up or posted) and a repack dated where no open accounting period covers the day are now refused in words ("No open accounting period covers 2030-01-01 ..."), as a write-off and an adjustment already were. **Only for a firm that has opened books**: a firm with no accounting period keeps any date, as before. A hold a goods receipt places for inspection is not asked again -- the receipt's own journal has already answered for the date. | #1373 | `tests/unit/test_inventory_round_4.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_dates.py` |
| D-STK-46 | 2026-10-08 | **Units a customer returned as scrap stood in no stock bucket** (Low; the owner's rule of 2026-10-08): the valuation carried them and nothing on screen could take them off. They now arrive in the damaged bucket with the damaged units of the same return, and a cancelled return takes them out again. **A write-off could not reach the damaged bucket at all** -- it drew on the shelf and on quarantine only, so damaged returns were stranded the same way. It now takes what a customer sent back damaged or as scrap first, then quarantine, then the shelf, and the figure in its refusal counts them. Goods written off as given to a customer never come out of the damaged bucket. Damage that arrived on a receipt or a transfer is in current stock and blocked there, as before, and is not counted twice. | #1373 | `tests/unit/test_inventory_round_4.py` |
| D-STK-53 | 2026-10-08 | **What a repack produced of a batch-tracked product went into no batch** (Low): a medicine on hand with no batch and no expiry, sold last and never short-dated, and breaking a kit did the same to a batch-tracked part. A produce line now names its batch: one the product has (`batch_id`, or its number), or a new number opened with its dates the way a goods receipt opens one, so a dated product is refused a new batch with no expiry. A line that names none is refused by product name; a batch named for a product that keeps none is refused too. The Repacking screen shows a Batch number box (and Expiry date, where the product is dated) on a produced line of such a product and on no other. **Breaking a kit** gives a batch-tracked part back to the batch this kit's most recent assembly in the warehouse drew it from, or to the batch the request names for it (`part_batches`); a kit never assembled there is refused, naming the part. **A kit that is itself kept in batches** is assembled into the batch the request names (`batch_number` and dates) and is no longer assembled by a delivery note on its own, which cannot name one. Decided by convention (ERPNext asks the batch of every manufactured item). | #1373 | `tests/unit/test_repack_names_the_batch_produced.py`; `desktop/test/repacking_test.dart`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_repack_batchless.py` |
| D-STK-54 | 2026-10-08 | **Stock standing in a bin was not seen by a delivery note line that named no bin** (Low): four in a bin and a note for four was refused *Insufficient available stock for dispatch line.*, and the refusal did not say the goods were there. A line naming no bin asks the warehouse: what stands on the warehouse's own row leaves first, where the order's hold is, and the rest is drawn from the bins by the product's issue rule across them (earliest expiry first, or first received), each movement naming the bin it left. Decided by convention: Tally and Busy pick from any location of a godown unless one is named, ERPNext asks the bin only where a putaway rule is set. A line that still falls short is refused with what stands free in each bin (*4 in bin B1*). **Not drawn from bins:** a unit with a serial number (it leaves from the bin named on the line, and the refusal says so) and a line whose batches a person chose. The order's hold stays on the warehouse's own row: goods in a bin are not held for an order that names no bin, so a note that names the bin can still take them first. | #1373 | `tests/unit/test_dispatch_draws_from_bins.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_bins.py` |
| D-STK-55 | 2026-10-09 | **Home's stock alerts listed ten rows of each kind in whatever order the database returned them** (Low; found by the round 4 drive, when the fixture firm passed ten low items and TC-STOCK-019's new one was counted and not listed): the count was right and the rows under it were not the ones to act on first, though the to-do says *the worst*. Each kind is now listed worst first -- the largest shortfall against the reorder level for low and out of stock, the largest excess over the maximum, the most in transit, the nearest expiry -- with the product code deciding a tie, so the list is the same on every read. The counts are unchanged. | #1374 | `tests/unit/test_stock_alerts.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/tc_stock_019.py` |
| D-STK-56 | 2026-10-09 | **An opening-stock import refused at posting left its draft behind** (Low, inventory; found by a new probe of round 8): `POST /inventory/opening-stock/import` with `auto_post` saved the draft and posted it in two commits, so a posting that was refused -- the item already has posted opening stock in that warehouse -- left a draft document holding the reference number, and the corrected payload under the same number was refused: *Opening stock reference number already exists.* The draft and its posting are now staged and committed once, so a refused import writes nothing and the number stays free. The route has no screen (Opening Stock's **Import from file** is `import-file`, which already wrote all or nothing). | #1379 | `tests/unit/test_inventory_foundation.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_opening_import_json.py` |
| D-STK-57 | 2026-10-09 | **The adjustment limit judged the number typed, not the pieces moved** (Medium, inventory; found by a new probe of round 9): a write-off or an adjustment sent with a pack unit (`entered_uom_id`) was valued for the role's limit at the typed quantity times the average cost of one piece, so three boxes of twelve at 60 a piece -- 2,160 of stock -- passed a limit of 500 as 180 and posted. A request for approval stated the same low worth, so the requester's own limit covered it and they approved it themselves. The limit and a request's stated worth are now read from the quantity in the stock unit (`InventoryService.moved_base_quantity`), and the request's quantity is the pieces it moves. No screen sends a pack unit on these two forms (both type pieces), so the gap was open to the API alone. | #1380 | `tests/unit/test_adjustment_approval.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_limit_in_packs.py` |
| D-STK-58 | 2026-10-09 | **An opening-stock CSV or XLSX with a cell that could not be read answered 500** (Low, inventory; found by a new probe of round 9): `POST /inventory/opening-stock/import` with `format=csv` or `xlsx` built each line by hand, so a product id that is not an id, a quantity of *lots* or below nought, a level that is not a number, a file with no rows, a file that is not text and a file that is not a workbook each answered *An unexpected error occurred.* Nothing was written. The cells now go through the request model as a JSON import's do, so each is a 422 naming its line and field (`lines[2].quantity: ...`), and a file that cannot be opened or holds no row with a product and a quantity is refused in a sentence. A CSV saved by Excel with a byte-order mark is read. The route has no screen (Opening Stock's **Import from file** is `import-file`, which named its rows already). | #1380 | `tests/unit/test_inventory_foundation.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_opening_import_csv.py` |
| D-STK-59 | 2026-10-09 | **A request for approval was judged at what it was worth on the day of asking** (Medium, inventory; found by a new probe of round 10): a write-off of 40 pieces asked for at an average cost of 10 said 400; after a dearer receipt took the average to 100 the same 40 pieces were worth 4,000, and the requester, limited to 500, approved it themselves, because `approve` compared the stored `estimated_value` with the approver's limit. Approval now values the request's pieces at the average cost of that moment, refuses on that figure by name, and keeps it on the approved request; the list of waiting requests shows the same present worth (one read for the page, nothing written), while a decided request keeps the figure it was decided at. | #1381 | `tests/unit/test_adjustment_approval.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_request_lifecycle.py` |
| D-STK-60 | 2026-10-09 | **A request that could never be posted was accepted, or refused as a conflict** (Low, inventory; same probe): a request naming a reason the firm does not keep was saved (201) and could then only be rejected; one naming a product or a warehouse that is not the firm's answered **409** *The request conflicts with existing data. Please retry.* -- the database's foreign key speaking, where a direct post says *Product does not belong to the active firm.* `submit` now checks the branch, warehouse, location, product, batch and reason the way the post does (`InventoryService.assert_postable`) and refuses with the post's own sentence (422), writing nothing. A quantity larger than the shelf holds is still accepted: the stock may arrive before the decision, and approval refuses while it has not. | #1381 | `tests/unit/test_adjustment_approval.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_request_lifecycle.py` |
| D-STK-61 | 2026-10-09 | **An evidence file with a name or a place of only spaces was kept** (Low, inventory; found by a new probe of round 10): `POST /inventory/transactions/{id}/attachments` (and the count's, and `attachments` on an adjustment, write-off or transfer) took `file_name` or `file_path` of three spaces, trimmed it and stored an empty one, so the movement listed a file with no name that nothing could open. Both are trimmed and refused when nothing is left (422, *Name the file and say where it is kept.*). The screens pick a real file, so only a caller of the API could send one. | #1381 | `tests/unit/test_stock_evidence.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_evidence_edges.py` |
| D-STK-62 | 2026-10-09 | **The ten-file cap on stock evidence was a cap on one request, not on the record** (Low, inventory; same probe): ten files were the most one request could carry, and a second request added ten more, so a movement or a count sheet could hold any number. Decided by the convention of Zoho Books and of this application's other documents (a document keeps a stated number of files): a stock movement or a count sheet keeps at most **10** files; a request that would pass it is refused by name with how many it holds (422), and removing one makes room. A transfer's two legs share one set, so they share the ten. | #1381 | `tests/unit/test_stock_evidence.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_evidence_edges.py` |

Stock moved at once while the journal was dated ahead, so until that day the trial balance's Inventory and the stock valuation's books figure disagreed by the value moved (60.00 on the fixture firm). Decided by the convention of ERPNext (no future-dated stock posting) since stock here moves when the movement is saved: a stock write dated after **the firm's own today** is refused, 422, "... cannot be dated ...: that is after today". Today and any earlier date in an open period are unchanged. Documents of buying and selling that move stock are not changed by this. | this merge | `tests/unit/test_adjustment_approval.py`; `docs/qa/checks/inventory/p_dated_ahead.py` |

The bill debits the line to *Purchases* (`PURCHASE_EXPENSE`) instead of leaving it to price variance, a purchase return of a service that never entered stock is refused by name (a debit note claims it), and a landed cost or Bill of Entry lands nothing on a receipt line with no movement behind it. | D-UI-5 | 2026-10-04 | **Save & New made one record and then rewrote it.** The CRUD dialog's create checkpoint kept the first record's id after a successful create, so after *Save & New* every later save in the same dialog skipped the create and sent the new form's assignments to the first record. A resource without assignments silently saved nothing. | D-TEST-2 | 2026-10-02 | Four desktop tests failed when the full suite ran on a loaded machine and passed alone. The five file-import dialog tests chose a file and slept a fixed 200 ms of real time while the dialog awaited `XFile.readAsBytes()`; under load the bytes had not arrived, *Check file* stayed disabled and the check found nothing. They now wait for the condition itself -- *Check file* enabled -- through one shared helper, `chooseImportFile` (`desktop/test/support/import_file.dart`), capped at 20 s. The lockout countdown test fixed the lock 90 s from the top of the test, and a loaded machine spent a second before the sign-in failed, so the screen read 01:29 for 01:30; the fake now stamps the lock when the sign-in fails, half a second short of 90. | D-QA-17 | 2026-09-25 | Approving a sales order converted from a quotation reserved 4 × QA-P1 in **STORE2** (holding none, available -4) while MAIN held 10. **Root cause, desktop:** `GET /warehouses` lists newest first, and the quotation editor defaulted its branch and warehouse to the first of each list, so the hand-added STORE2 became where every new quote shipped from. The conversion copied it and approval reserved there, correctly, because that is what the order named. The "no warehouse" seen on the laptop was the order view: its header never passed the branch or warehouse, so it printed `-` for every order. The server was never guessing: `warehouse_id` is required and NOT NULL on the quotation and order headers, validated against the branch, and a line with none takes the header's; the MAIN made by the Set up panel is `is_default`. **Fix:** one rule, `preferredBranchId` / `preferredWarehouseId` (`desktop/lib/models/branch_warehouse.dart`): the firm's default branch and *that branch's* default warehouse, the only one when there is one, otherwise nothing, and the form asks ("Choose a warehouse."). The quotation and sales order editors use it, and changing the branch re-picks that branch's warehouse (the quotation offers only that branch's). The purchase order and sales return editors took the first (newest) warehouse the same way and now take the default too; the purchase form keeps the first only where no default is marked, because it displays the first item of an unset field. The sales order view prints its branch and warehouse, the invoice view its branch. **Decided by Claude, industry standard:** a document's warehouse defaults from configuration (the branch's default) and is otherwise chosen, never taken from list order; the server keeps requiring it, so nothing downstream ever guesses. An approved order cannot be edited, so a tester's order already on STORE2 is repaired by cancelling it (which releases the reservation) and raising the order again directly (a quotation converts once). Approval still reserving beyond on-hand is the back-order design (the shortfall report lists it), not changed here. | #657 | `desktop/test/document_warehouse_default_test.dart` (all five); `test_quotation_module.py::test_approving_a_converted_order_reserves_in_the_quoted_warehouse`, `::test_a_quotation_must_name_where_it_ships_from` |

Both are kept in `ports.json`; the port reaches the service definition, the firewall rule (renamed `AgencyPlatformServer-TCP`, the old name removed), the health wait and `branding.json`. Our own database is recognised by `.env` pointing at localhost on any port of the range. Driven: `CheckPort` exits 0 on a free port and 3 naming `pycharm64.exe` on a held one; the .iss compiles. Not yet driven by a full install. | #845 | none automated -- installer QA rows A15a, A15b in `docs/INSTALLER_QA_CHECKLIST.md` |

Found by the first laptop install (service registered, `/health` never answered); reproduced in isolation -- `type` aliases and generic functions compile and run, any PEP 695 *class* leaks the attribute. `ApiResponse`, `PaginatedResponse`, `ReportRows` and `BaseRepository` now use `TypeVar` + `Generic[T]`. | #652 | `test_no_generic_class_syntax.py` (AST: no class under `app/` carries type parameters) |

| D-IDN-9 | 2026-09-24 | A role code is unique among live roles in its own scope: the platform-wide roles (system roles included) share one namespace and each firm has its own. `UQ_roles_code` became `UQ_roles_platform_code_active` and `UQ_roles_firm_code_active`, partial over live rows with `sqlite_where` beside `postgresql_where` (migration `20260924_0161`, platform only, guarded and idempotent), and `create_role` asks the same question, so a deleted role releases its code and one firm is no longer told what another called its roles. A firm role still may not take a system role's code (the reserved-code check), since code elsewhere recognises a seeded role by it. Decided by Claude, industry standard: role names are tenant-scoped in multi-tenant IAM. | #643 | `test_identity_low_rows.py` (3 tests) |
| D-IDN-10 | 2026-09-24 | Every item. The eleven unenforced codes are **designation-only** (`DESIGNATION_ONLY_PERMISSION_CODES`): no seeded role holds them, `set_role_permissions` refuses them by name, a firm is not shown them, seeding retires a stray grant and `20260924_0161` retired every existing one. Decided by Claude, industry standard: tenant lifecycle, the capability catalogue and setting somebody else's password are operator (superuser) actions, not grantable permissions, and a lockout expires on its own while switching an account off is `USER_UPDATE`; the firm routes stay platform-only as `test_platform_only_routes.py` pins, and the codes stay in the catalogue because the designation's claim carries them and the desktop gates the Firms and Permissions buttons on them. `GET /users/{id}/global-roles` resolves the user in the caller's firm first (404 outside it). `/firm-members` and the member count leave out switched-off accounts. `_assert_exclusive_firm_user` counts any live membership elsewhere, suspended or not. Refresh reuse detection fires only for a **rotated** token presented again more than 30 s after rotation, so signing out or two refreshes racing no longer signs the person out everywhere (decided by Claude, industry standard: the Auth0/Okta rotation grace period). Sign-out now ends the access token too: it carries `sid`, the refresh token issued beside it, and `get_current_principal` refuses it once that session is signed out. Firm `PUT` dumps only what was sent (an omitted `is_active`, GST or PAN is kept; an explicit null still clears), and `status` is `ACTIVE`/`INACTIVE` mirroring `is_active` -- a status alone sets the flag, a disagreeing pair is refused, and the migration aligns existing rows. `POST /api/v1/firms/{id}/restore` brings a deleted firm back (platform-only, refused if a live firm took its code, GST or PAN). `user_templates` and `user_template_roles` joined `_PLATFORM_TABLES`, and the migration drops the stray copies from firm stores. `ACCESS_CONTROL_FRAMEWORK.md` Part 4 now says a global custom role lands in `permissions` only. `GET /permissions/{id}` takes `PERMISSION_VIEW` like the list. | #643 | `test_identity_low_rows.py` (15 tests), `test_identity_hardening.py`, `test_platform_only_routes.py`, `test_firms_module.py` |
| D-MST-11 | 2026-09-24 | Every item. Every master code -- and the customer's GST and PAN, the vendor's GSTIN, the category, type and storage-node names -- is unique among live rows: 22 plain keys became partial indexes (migration `20260924_0161`, idempotent, guarded with `has_table` on every store), and the services ask the same question through `assert_codes_free` (`app/common/master_codes.py`), so a deleted code is released and a clash is refused with a sentence naming it, not the bare 409; restoring a customer, vendor, branch, warehouse or product into a code a live row took since is refused by name. `UQ_products_firm_barcode_active` declares `sqlite_where`. Vendor categories and types, branch and warehouse types, a product category's create and edit, a storage-node edit and a vendor duplicate each write an audit row, and the four category/type edits no longer un-delete a retired row (a retired one is not found). A product category cannot be moved under itself or its own descendant, a move or recode rebuilds every descendant's path and level, and `delete_category` refuses a category named as a sub-category or holding live children. A display name is kept when an edit does not send one, and follows the name only when it was the name (`app/common/display_names.py`). Vendor categories and types, branch and warehouse types and product categories publish `ETag` and a `version` field and take `If-Match`. A second vendor duplicate counts up (`-COPY-1`) like a product's. The product list searches and pages in SQL. A module's own type cannot be renamed or deleted, its lifecycle states take only name, description and order edits and cannot be added or removed, any type with series or recorded events is refused delete, and a switched-off type issues no number. Decided by Claude, industry standard: system document types and their lifecycle are locked and switched off rather than deleted; the state flags describe the module's code and are not configuration it reads. Every history row is stamped strictly later than the one before it, so an edit's two rows no longer share PostgreSQL's transaction-start `now()`. | #621 | `test_a_supplier_advance_can_be_applied_to_a_bill_that_arrived_since`, `test_a_refund_on_the_books_cannot_be_applied_afterwards_either`, `test_receiving_writes_the_orders_own_history_row`, `test_an_edits_two_history_rows_are_in_order_on_the_clock`, and the desktop's `a supplier advance can be applied to a bill that arrived since` |

Existing stored invoices are not rewritten. Decided by Claude, industry standard: history is corrected by a credit/debit note, never by rewriting a posted bill. | #645 | `test_freight_agreed_with_the_goods_is_billed_not_only_taxed` |

A return raised on a delivery note is traced to the bill that billed it. `loyalty_entries.source_type` / `.source_id` (migration `20261005_0317`). | #1146 | `test_a_return_takes_back_the_points_its_value_earned`, `test_cancelling_the_return_gives_the_points_back_once` in `test_loyalty.py`; driven live on `fx_t1005j1us_s` |

| D-SELL-2 | 2026-09-19 | Cancelling an invoice kept the points it earned and their accrual. Owner decided: cancel takes back only the points still unspent, with their cost reversed; spent points stay spent. | #460 | `test_cancelling_a_bill_takes_its_points_and_their_accrual_back` and four more |
| D-SELL-3 | 2026-09-19 | An invoice could bill a delivery note that never dispatched. Only a dispatched note can be billed. | #462 | `test_a_note_whose_goods_never_left_cannot_be_billed` |
| D-SELL-4 | 2026-09-19 | Closing an approved note that never dispatched made it count as delivered and billable. Only a dispatched note can be closed. | #464 | `test_an_approved_note_that_never_dispatched_cannot_be_closed` |
| D-SELL-5 | 2026-09-19 | A hold stopped only a note's save. Edit, approve, dispatch and complete all refuse a held order; a held order's draft can still have its vehicle or remarks put right. | #466 | `test_a_note_approved_before_the_hold_does_not_ship` and two more |
| D-SELL-6 | 2026-09-19 | A return could be raised against a draft or undispatched note, or a draft or cancelled invoice. Only goods that left can come back. | #468 | `test_goods_that_never_left_cannot_come_back` and three more |
| D-SELL-7 | 2026-09-19 | The same goods could be returned twice, once through the note and once through the bill. Each route counts the other's returns; a bill whose goods came back through the note cannot be cancelled. | #469 | `test_goods_back_through_the_note_are_not_returned_again_through_the_bill` and two more |
| D-SELL-8 | 2026-09-19 | A receipt whose advance had been applied could not be reversed (driven: 422 "overtaken"). Reversal undoes every receivable row the settlement wrote, newest first. | #471 | `test_a_receipt_whose_advance_was_applied_reverses_in_full` |
| D-SELL-9 | 2026-09-19 | Converting a quotation skipped the promotion claim. Decided by Claude, industry standard (Odoo re-checks offers at confirmation): only what was typed on the quote carries over as typed; everything the pricing rule worked out is worked out again by the order on its own date, which stages the claim. | #482 | `test_a_converted_order_claims_the_offer_it_was_quoted`, `test_a_discount_typed_on_the_quotation_carries_over_as_typed` |
| D-SELL-10 | 2026-09-19 | Record Receipt offered a bill's full remainder after a return or credit note against it. Decided by Claude, mirrors D-BUY-6: a return raised on the bill's lines and an approved credit note come off the bill; a return raised on a note stays a credit on the account. One `credited_against()` serves receipts and the loyalty cap. | #474 | `test_returns_and_credit_notes_against_a_bill_come_off_what_it_owes`, `test_points_cannot_settle_what_a_credit_note_already_took_off` |
| D-SELL-11 | 2026-09-19 | An order could be cancelled after it shipped or was billed, and a note raised against a CLOSED order. Decided by Claude, industry standard (SAP B1, Odoo): a shipped order is closed, not cancelled; a note moves only while its order is open. | #475 | `test_an_order_that_has_shipped_cannot_be_cancelled` and three more; `document_status_gate_test.dart` |
| D-SELL-12 | 2026-09-19 | A sales invoice could be closed from DRAFT or CANCELLED. Only an approved invoice can be closed. | #463 | `test_only_an_approved_invoice_can_be_closed`, `test_a_cancelled_invoice_cannot_be_closed` |
| D-SELL-13 | 2026-09-19 | With the delivery-note stage automatic, saving a draft invoice dispatched the goods. Decided by Claude, industry standard: stock leaves at invoice approval, in the approval's transaction; cancelling the draft withdraws the note it raised. | #478 | `test_a_draft_bill_ships_nothing_until_it_is_approved` and two more |
| D-SELL-14 | 2026-09-19 | Converting a quotation was two commits, so a failure could leave an order behind a quotation still OPEN. It commits once. | #483 | `test_a_conversion_that_fails_half_way_leaves_nothing_behind` |
| D-SELL-15 | 2026-09-19 | An offer's claim limit was counted per row, so editing the offer reset it (driven: limit 1, two approvals). Claims count across the version group, which is locked whole. | #479 | `test_approval_counts_claims_on_every_version_of_the_offer`, coupon twin |
| D-SELL-16 | 2026-09-19 | A proforma left out freight and the order's charges (driven: order 375.81, proforma 300.41). It states what the order will bill; `other_charges` is derived and shown on the desktop. | #484 | `test_freight_and_the_order_s_charges_reach_the_total`; Dart test |
| D-SELL-17 | 2026-09-19 | Proformas and purchase invoices shared the `PI` prefix. Decided by Claude: proformas number as `PF`; a firm still on the old default moves once, with an audit row, and its counter carries on so nothing is renumbered. | #485 | `test_a_proforma_never_shares_the_purchase_invoice_s_prefix`, `test_a_firm_that_chooses_pi_afterwards_keeps_it` |
| D-SELL-18 | 2026-09-19 | Selling pickers could not reach older records (the D-BUY-11 twins). Billable documents, the return/credit-note source picker, the settlement party list and the proforma, quotation and direct-bill pickers read every page. | #487 | `desktop/test/selling_pickers_read_every_page_test.dart`; 2 backend tests |
| D-SELL-19 | 2026-09-19 | Spending goodwill points debited Loyalty Payable for value never accrued. Decided by Claude, industry standard (IFRS 15 / Odoo): every point held is a liability -- an adjustment posts Dr 5700 / Cr 2600 (and back) at the scheme's value. Goodwill granted before the fix and still held is trued up by `scripts/repair_legacy_rows.py` -- one journal per firm, Dr 5700 / Cr 2600, reference `LOY-GOODWILL-TRUEUP-<firm code>`, booked in the current period (D-DATA-1). Decided by Claude, industry standard. | #477, #644 | `test_goodwill_points_are_owed_before_they_are_spent` |
| D-SELL-20 | 2026-09-19 | Points could not be spent on one bill twice (409, duplicate journal reference). Later redemptions take `-2`, `-3`. | #476 | `test_points_can_be_spent_on_one_bill_more_than_once` |
| D-SELL-21 | 2026-09-19 | A sales return worked its tax out again through today's rules (driven: a rate cut made a return of goods billed at 18% reverse 12%). Decided by Claude, GST credit-note rule: a return reverses the tax and components the bill charged, pro rata. | #481 | `test_a_return_reverses_the_tax_the_bill_charged`, `test_a_return_raised_on_the_bill_reverses_its_tax_too` |
| D-SELL-29 | 2026-09-19 | A return could lift its own quantity cap with `allow_over_return` in the request (driven: 50 returned against a note of 5, credited 4,832.10; purchase twin: 40 against a receipt of 4). Decided by Claude, industry standard: no over-return tolerance; the fields are gone and the cap always applies. | #470, #472 | `test_a_return_cannot_lift_its_own_cap` (sales and purchase) |
| D-STK-15 | 2026-09-19 | The desktop sales-invoice editor had no serial picker, so a serial-tracked bill could not be raised with the delivery-note stage automatic. The editor picks serials like the note editor. | #489 | backend test + 2 Dart tests |
| D-STK-16 | 2026-10-03 | A delivery note refused to ship stock its own order held: the gate compared stock free of every reservation -- this order's included -- with the line, though the dispatch releases that reservation in the same step, so a product fully reserved by the order it was shipping read as short. The gate now counts the line's own hold. Found building kits (STK-15). | STK-15 PR | `test_a_note_shipping_more_packs_than_assembled_assembles_the_rest` |
| BL-31.13 | 2026-09-19 | The Stock Ledger's type filter offered types the server never writes and left out real ones. It offers exactly the written types, with labels; a guard fails on drift. | #455 | `test_stock_ledger_types_match_the_server.py`, `inventory_stale_filter_test.dart` |
| D-BUY-7 | 2026-09-19 | Billing part of a receipt line cleared the whole line's accrual (TEST01: a bill for 4 of 10 cleared 2300 by 1000.00 and booked 600.00 of false gain to 5400). Each bill now clears its share by quantity, and the bill that completes the receipt takes the rounding residual so the bills net exactly to what the receipt accrued. Existing over-cleared postings are not repaired. | #446 | `test_billing_part_of_a_receipt_clears_only_its_share_of_the_accrual`, `test_the_bill_that_completes_a_receipt_takes_the_rounding_residual` |

| BL-31.1, 31.2, 31.4-31.8, 31.10-31.12 | 2026-09-09 to 09-12 | Found in manual testing and fixed the same day; each is written up in `docs/BACKLOG.md` §31. | see §31 | see §31 |

Defects found and fixed during the manual-testing runs of 2026-09-13 to 09-15
(#336-#413) are recorded in each PR and in the plan-correction PRs for their
section; they were not given ids, and are not repeated here.
| D-MST-1 | 2026-09-20 | A product holding stock, a reservation or a document in flight was deleted, and its stock then invisible and unmovable (driven: 45 in MAIN and 5 in a bin gone from every screen). Refused by quantity and place, singly and in bulk, checking every row before touching one; quotations and proformas do not hold it. Decided by Claude, industry standard: refuse, never cascade; "open" means still to move stock or be billed, so CANCELLED and CLOSED never hold. TEST01's 13 already-deleted products holding 432 units are an owner data question. | #556 | `test_product_delete_guard.py` (6 tests) |
| D-MST-2 | 2026-09-20 | A customer's standing discount was writable with `CUSTOMER_UPDATE` alone (driven: SALES_MANAGER gave 100% and billed two at 0.00). The rate now takes `CUSTOMER_MANAGE_SETTINGS`, like the credit limit (D-CFG-17), in both directions. Assigning a customer to an existing segment stays with `CUSTOMER_UPDATE`: choosing among approved tiers, not setting a price. | #557 | `test_a_standing_discount_moves_only_for_whoever_sets_the_prices` |
| D-MST-3 | 2026-09-20 | Another firm's segment, category, type or profile was accepted in the shared store and the segment's discount applied (driven: TESTSH1 priced at TESTSH2's 50%). Every master reference must be a live row of the same firm, refused as not found; on update only a reference that changes is judged, so a record holding a since-retired id still saves. Existing cross-firm references are set to NULL by `scripts/repair_legacy_rows.py` (D-DATA-1). Decided by Claude, industry standard: a reference to another tenant's master is invalid data, and NULL is how the services read "none". | #559, #644 | `test_master_references_same_firm.py` (5 tests) |
| D-MST-4 | 2026-09-20 | A customer under an approved order or a vendor with unbilled receipts was deleted (driven: GRNI holding 1,000.00 for a vendor who no longer existed). A document holds its party while it still has something to do -- an order not fully delivered, a note or receipt not yet billed, a return not closed -- named by number; a fully delivered and billed order does not, or every trading customer would be undeletable. | #560 | `test_party_delete_open_documents.py` (4 tests) |
| D-MST-5 | 2026-09-20 | A product update dumped its whole write model, clearing every omitted field, the custom fields and the images (driven: a three-field PUT left the product with no category, tax group, units or price). `exclude_unset` on update, child collections on `model_fields_set`, row fallbacks; a caller without the cost code can neither see nor write the cost. The form never sent `sub_category_id`, so every form save had been clearing it; it no longer does. | #561 | `test_product_partial_update.py` (4 tests) |
| D-MST-6 | 2026-09-20 | An INACTIVE or ON_HOLD customer still took new quotations and orders (driven: SO created and approved for an INACTIVE customer). Only ACTIVE takes a new document, mirroring the vendor check; what is in flight carries on, and converting an accepted quotation counts as new. The product half is D-MST-12. | #562 | `test_inactive_customer_new_sales.py` (6 tests) |
| D-MST-7 | 2026-09-20 | A product's base unit and tracking mode changed under stock (driven: PIECE to BOX with 50 on hand, and serial tracking switched on over untracked units). Both are settled before the first receipt and change only once the product is back at nothing, in either direction; a full form resending what is stored still saves; naming a unit where there was none is a repair, not a change. | #563 | `test_product_unit_change_under_stock.py` (6 tests) |
| D-MST-8 | 2026-09-20 | A storage bin holding stock was deleted and the stock could not be moved back out (driven: a bin holding 5). Refused by quantity, and by an open document line naming it; the warehouse and branch guards gained the same open-document check; the delete is audited. | #564 | `test_storage_area_delete_guard.py` (5 tests) |
| D-MST-9 | 2026-09-20 | The product import committed per row, so a clash on the second row left the first written and the corrected file was then a duplicate (driven). All three formats stage and commit once; a bad number or status names its row; the whole file is refused at the first bad row, as the other four imports do. | #566 | `test_product_import_one_transaction.py` (5 tests) |
| D-MST-10 | 2026-09-20 | Five seeded master codes were enforced by no route. `VENDOR_MANAGE_BANK_DETAILS` gates writing where a supplier is paid, `VENDOR_VIEW_FINANCIAL_DETAILS` gates the accounts in the response (a VIEWER now sees a vendor with no accounts), and a product's price, tax group and custom fields each need `PRODUCT_PRICING_MANAGE`, `PRODUCT_TAX_MANAGE` or `PRODUCT_ATTRIBUTE_MANAGE`, as a projection of the principal in the service like `PRODUCT_VIEW_COST_PRICE`. A number resent as a form types it is not a change. Migration `20260919_0150` grants ACCOUNTANT the vendor read. | #569 | `test_master_duty_codes_are_enforced.py` (5 tests) |
| D-MST-12 | 2026-09-24 | `products.status` was unread on the sales side -- the product half of D-MST-6, which #562 fixed for the customer only -- so an INACTIVE product was still quoted, ordered and billed. `assert_product_takes_new_lines` mirrors the customer helper: only ACTIVE sells, DRAFT/INACTIVE/ARCHIVED are refused by code and name, at create and at edit, on every quotation line and every sales order line -- which is also every converted quotation and every bare bill, since the chain turns one into an order, and the refusal names what was being raised. Decided by Claude, industry standard: a line inherited from a delivery note or an order already agreed is **not** refused, so what was promised is still delivered and billed; a promotion's gift line is configuration rather than a typed line, and is not judged. | #620 | `test_quotation_module.py`, `test_sales_order_module.py`, `test_sales_invoice_module.py` (10 tests) |
| D-TER-1 | 2026-09-20 | A margin rule was accepted and stored as a value rule: `measure` was declared and never assigned. Written, read back and audited; an edit silent about it leaves it. | #567 | `test_margin_commission.py` (2 tests) |
| D-TER-2 | 2026-09-20 | Two targets over the same days counted the same sales twice and the bonus was paid on the total (driven: a monthly and a quarterly both met). Two targets for one scope on the same basis may not overlap at all, refused by name; the other basis may; the bonus is judged per basis and met only when every basis is. | #568 | `test_a_target_overlapping_another_on_the_same_basis_is_refused`, `test_targets_on_different_bases_are_judged_each_on_their_own` |
| D-TER-3 | 2026-09-20 | A credit note, a return or a refund took nothing off commission, the invoiced figure or a target (driven: 2,000 credited, 500 still paid). One walk, `settlements/services/net_sales.py`, is what the commission report and the target achievement both read; a credit belongs to the sale it credits, comes off the latest receipts first, and a PAID period's shortfall is carried as `clawback_amount` on the next accrual, oldest first, never taking more than the new period earned (migration `20260920_0150`). Decided by Claude, industry standard: the paid row is never rewritten. | #570 | `test_commission.py` (4 tests), `test_commission_payouts.py` (4 tests) |
| D-TER-4 | 2026-09-20 | One person accrued, adjusted, approved and paid their own payout (driven: +5,000.00 "because"). Nobody approves or pays their own; the accruer cannot approve; the payer must differ from the approver; the row records `approved_by`, `approved_at`, `paid_by` (migration `20260920_0151`) and cancel clears none of them. Decided by Claude, industry standard: maker-checker per person, not per role; 403. The ceiling and the payee's own draft are D-TER-20. | #580 | `test_commission_payouts.py` (4 tests) |
| D-TER-5 | 2026-09-20 | A payout was paid from whatever account, on whatever date, the caller named (driven: Commission Payable itself). `method` CASH or BANK resolves the firm's control account like a receipt; an account named outright must be one of the two; `paid_on` may not precede `accrued_on`; the desktop asks Bank / Cash. | #576 | `test_commission_payouts.py` (3 tests) |
| D-TER-6 | 2026-09-20 | A period was accrued before it ended, so money landing afterwards belonged to no payout, and an advance applied months later was counted in the month it arrived (driven both ways). A payout is accrued only once `period_end` is before today (UTC), booked between the period's end and today; `settlement_allocations.allocated_on` (migration `20260920_0152`, backfilled where NULL) dates a collection by the day it met the bill, `max(settlement_date, invoice_date)` for a late application; `net_sales` reads it, so credits, collections, clawbacks and targets share one clock. Decided by Claude: any period length stays allowed because the overlap key already refuses double-counting. Decided by Claude, industry standard: `allocated_on` stays derived as max(settlement_date, invoice_date); a caller does not choose the date an allocation takes effect. | #582 | `test_commission_payouts.py` (3 tests), `test_settlements.py` (1 test) |
| D-TER-7 | 2026-09-20 | A territory edit that left out `route_profile` deleted the route, and a beat-plan edit that left out its stops cleared them (driven). Both updates are their own optional schemas dumped with `exclude_unset`; omitted keeps, explicit null clears; validated on the merged row; audited before/after. The desktop's zone editor now sends `route_profile: null` deliberately. | #571 | `test_an_edit_that_leaves_out_the_route_profile_keeps_the_route`, `test_an_edit_that_leaves_out_the_stops_keeps_them` |
| D-TER-8 | 2026-09-20 | A target edit that left out `salesman_id` made it the firm's target and stopped the person's bonus (driven: PUT of dates and amount answered `salesman_id: null`). `SalesTargetUpdate`, every field optional, dumped with `exclude_unset`; explicit null clears the person, territory or notes and is refused on a column a target cannot do without; the overlap check reads the merged row; a no-op edit writes nothing. Decided by Claude: `status` stays writable, it is an in-force flag with no transition endpoint. | #579 | `test_sales_targets.py` (6 tests) |
| D-TER-9 | 2026-09-20 | A route the caller named was kept as sent (driven: a closed round, another firm's round). `validate_named_route` refuses one that is not this firm's, not in force on the document's date, or not the customer's; the invoice checks a route typed on the bill. | #572 | `test_sales_scope_resolution.py` (4 tests), `test_delivery_note_module.py` |
| D-TER-10 | 2026-09-20 | A shop could not go back on a round it was once primary on (driven: bare 409). A rejoining shop is decided like a new row; naming a round primary demotes the others first, before the row is written, because the key is checked per statement. | #573 | `test_a_shop_can_go_back_on_a_round_it_was_once_primary_on`, `test_naming_a_round_primary_moves_the_flag_there` |
| D-TER-11 | 2026-09-20 | Somebody who had left the firm was still derived on to new orders, which then could not be delivered (driven). Derivation and inheritance keep only active members, read once per call through `FirmMetadataReader`; the round's other assignee takes over if there is exactly one, else nobody. The rows themselves are D-TER-17. | #575 | `test_sales_scope_resolution.py` (3 tests) |
| D-TER-12 | 2026-09-20 | A target on a territory or a region achieved nothing (driven: 5,000.00 on a region read 0.00 against 10,620.00 on its routes). A target covers its node and every live descendant, walked by `parent_id` rather than a path prefix; the achievement row says which territory; a person's target on a region judges on the same scope. Decided by Claude: a region target and a route target beneath it are different scopes and may overlap. | #581 | `test_sales_targets.py` (4 tests) |
| D-TER-13 | 2026-09-20 | Copying a subtree committed node by node and found the subtree by `path LIKE`, which took the sibling `T-N2` under `T-N` and read `_` as a wildcard (driven). Walked by `parent_id`, staged with `commit=False`, one audit row, one commit: a refusal anywhere writes nothing. | #574 | `test_copying_a_subtree_takes_its_children_and_not_a_sibling_s`, `test_a_copy_refused_partway_writes_nothing` |
| D-DSK-CF-1 | 2026-10-03 | A form closed before its custom-field definitions arrived raised an uncaught zone error (`A CustomFieldsController was used after being disposed`), seen in the phase 2 client's log at launch. `CustomFieldsController.start` now stops when the form has gone; `desktop/test/custom_fields_test.dart` fails without the fix. | #1056 |
| D-PERF-2 | 2026-10-03 | The app loaded slowly and Quotations spun for ~40 s during the owner's test: a firm request holds up to three pooled connections and the pool held 15, so the ~30 reads Home opens together each took one and waited for the next -- a pool deadlock ended only by the 30 s timeout (30 parallel requests gave 30 × 503). `ConnectionBudgetMiddleware` (`backend/app/core/middleware/connection_budget.py`) admits (pool_size + max_overflow) // 3 requests at once; the rest queue holding nothing. | #1058 |

## Not a defect

| Id | Decided | Summary | Reason |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| D-UI-15 | 2026-10-07 | A sales return for more than went out showed no message about the quantity (SCRQ-6 in `docs/qa/SCREEN_FLOW_CHECK_ROUND_1_2026-10-07.md`). | Not a defect. The return editor already shows "only N went out" and marks the box; the flow had read the screen in the wrong place. `return_over_quantity_message_test.dart` pins it (#1326). |
| D-UI-18 | 2026-10-07 | The bill editor's "Choose the customer first" hint seemed to linger after a customer was chosen (SCRQ-1 in `docs/qa/SCREEN_FLOW_CHECK_ROUND_1_2026-10-07.md`). | Not a defect. The hint fades out once a customer is chosen and only stays in the widget tree. `sales_invoice_several_notes_test.dart` pins it (#1326). |
| D-UI-35 | 2026-10-07 | Send is not offered on a recorded receipt, where the case book expected it (SCRQ-35, SC-RC-008 in `docs/qa/SCREEN_FLOW_CHECK_ROUND_2_2026-10-07.md`). | Not a defect. A receipt offers Print and Files; the book was wrong and is corrected. |
| D-UI-36 | 2026-10-07 | The receipts grid does not show Cash or Bank, On Account and Other Deductions (SCRQ-36, SC-RC-001 in `docs/qa/SCREEN_FLOW_CHECK_ROUND_2_2026-10-07.md`). | Not a defect. The grid's columns are Number, Customer, Date, Method, Cleared, Status, Amount; the book is corrected. |
| D-UI-43 | 2026-10-07 | The purchase order cards are not the ones the case book lists, and Restore does not bring a Cancelled order back (SCRQ-43, SC-PO-001 and SC-PO-014 in `docs/qa/SCREEN_FLOW_CHECK_ROUND_2_2026-10-07.md`). | Not a defect. The cards are All, Draft, Open, Cancelled, Closed; Restore is for a deleted order, and a cancelled order is raised again rather than revived. The book is corrected. |
| D-UI-45 | 2026-10-07 | A second Draft bill with the same supplier and the same supplier bill number is accepted (SCRQ-45, SC-PB-021 in `docs/qa/SCREEN_FLOW_CHECK_ROUND_2_2026-10-07.md`). | Not a defect. A repeated number is a warning by design (`_duplicate_warning` in the purchase invoice service, shown in the bill editor), because suppliers start their numbers again each year. The book is corrected. |
| D-UI-47 | 2026-10-07 | The purchase invoice grid has no Due Date and no Outstanding column (SCRQ-47, SC-PB-001 in `docs/qa/SCREEN_FLOW_CHECK_ROUND_2_2026-10-07.md`). | Not a defect. The columns are Invoice Number, Files, Supplier, Supplier Invoice, Invoice Date, Status, Grand Total; what is owed is on Payables. The book is corrected. |
| D-PRC-55 | 2026-10-06 | A draft bill line typed in pieces is refused when it is sent back exactly as the read returned it (PRCQ-55, sales and purchase). | Decided by Claude: by design, a known limit. The API contract is to send `entered_quantity`'s figure with its unit, as the desktop does; the read alone does not carry both. Found in round 4 (`docs/qa/PRICING_API_CHECK_ROUND_4_2026-10-06.md`). |
| D-STK-42 | 2026-10-08 | `If-Match` is not read on `PUT /inventory/counts/{id}`: a count sheet saved from an older copy is accepted (F15 in `docs/qa/INVENTORY_API_CHECK_ROUND_1_2026-10-08.md`). | Decided by the owner on 2026-10-08: left as it is, a known limit. Two people fill one sheet, and a save writes only the lines it names, matched on product, batch and storage location, so two counters on different lines never overwrite each other; refusing the second save would send one of them back to retype a shelf. Two saves of the *same* line keep the later figure, and the trail records both. Posting the sheet is a separate step. `p_stale_version.py` pins the behaviour. The transfer half of the row is fixed (see *Fixed*). |
| D-STK-20 | 2026-10-08 | The stock account can sit a paisa from the stock valuation after several dispatches at an average cost with more than two decimals: three notes at 55.452127 each credited 1200 with 55.45 (166.35 where 166.356 was moved). Seen on one pharmacy firm in each of buying and selling rounds 6, 7 and 8 (`docs/qa/BUYING_SELLING_API_CHECK_ROUND_8_2026-10-06.md`). | Decided by Claude in inventory round 4: a known limit. Stock is valued to four places and the ledger posts two, so each movement's journal is the rounded share of a figure the valuation keeps whole. The difference is paise per product, does not grow with turnover in one direction, and comes back when the stock is sold out (a product at nil holds no value); the trial balance always balances. Closing it means valuing every movement at two places and carrying the average from the rounded total, which changes cost of goods, margin and every reversal for data already posted. Tally and Busy carry the same paise between stock summary and ledger. `p_books_agree.py` allows it. |
| D-PRC-56 | 2026-10-08 | The stock valuation report and the stock account part by a paisa where the average cost is not a round figure: 0.02 to 0.11 on the pricing fixture firms in round 10; a product kept in BOX and sold in PIECE shows it as a 2-paisa cost difference (0.5833 BOX at 720.00 is 419.98 where 420.00 was typed) that comes back when the box is finished (PRCQ-56, `docs/qa/PRICING_API_CHECK_ROUND_10_2026-10-07.md`). | Decided by Claude in inventory round 4: a known limit, the same behaviour and the same reason as D-STK-20 above. |
| D-SELL-86 | 2026-10-06 | Cancelling an approved counter bill keeps its coupon's offer claim: with an offer limited to 1, the row stays CLAIMED and a new draft is priced without it. | Decided by Claude, industry standard: kept. Cancelling an approved bill does not bring the goods back; its delivery note stays DISPATCHED and its order DELIVERED, and the claim belongs to that order, which still stands. The offer was used on goods the customer has. Cancelling an order that has not shipped does reverse its claim. Found live in round 6 (R6-4 in `docs/qa/BUYING_SELLING_API_CHECK_ROUND_6_2026-10-06.md`). |
| D-BUY-60 | 2026-10-05 | **The desktop purchase bill and purchase return editors send no `If-Match`**, so D-BUY-54's refusal of a stale save protects API clients and the requisition editor but not those two screens. | Not a defect. The desktop never updates a saved purchase bill or purchase return: both editors only create (`createPurchaseInvoice`, `create('purchase-returns', ...)`), and no code under `desktop/lib` sends a PUT to either route, so there is no stale save for `If-Match` to guard. The note on #1187 that raised it was wrong. Checked by two agents and by grep on 2026-10-05. Editing a draft bill or return on the desktop would be a feature; if it is built, `ApiClient.update` already takes `expectedVersion`. |
| D-BUY-31 | 2026-10-04 | A purchase return made before the goods were billed shows a total with GST (PR-2026-2027-000002: 236.00) while its journal posts 200 at cost with no tax. | Decided by Claude, industry standard: kept. The return document states the goods' value as the supplier priced them, tax included, as ERPNext's purchase-receipt return and Tally's rejection-out do; the ledger is what moves, and before billing it moves only GRNI and stock at cost (D-BUY-26). Found in purchasing round 2. |
| D-SELL-45 | 2026-10-04 | Sales invoices and delivery notes are numbered `SI-26-27-000001` / `DN-26-27-000001` while orders and purchase documents read `PO-QA01-HO-2026-2027-000003`. | Not a defect. CGST rule 46(b) (and 53, 55) caps a tax invoice, credit note or delivery challan number at 16 characters, so the GST-numbered types print the short year and leave out the firm and branch codes (`gst_numbering.py`, GST-2); orders and purchase documents are not GST serial numbers. Found in purchasing round 2. |
| D-SELL-46 | 2026-10-04 | The audit trail names the first person to raise a sales order as having created the firm's sales-order type, states and numbering rule (fieldsales@ on QA01). | Not a defect. A document type a firm has never used is set up on first use, inside that request, and the trail truthfully records whose request it was. Found in purchasing round 2. |
| D-DATA-5 | 2026-10-01 | `verify_sample_data.py` still reports three of the 44 local stores after the repair: TEST01 (PI-2026-2027-000008, a zero-value bill closed from DRAFT), `fx_t0919psxt_s` (two sales invoices closed from DRAFT or cancelled) and `fx_t0919fjt2_r` (a hand-reversed receipt journal, receivable rows with no journal, hand journals on 1100/1200/2300). | Not a defect on main. Every row was written while driving D-BUY-12, D-SELL-12, D-FIN-2, D-FIN-4 and D-FIN-11 by hand on 2026-09-18/19, before each fix merged, in throwaway fixture stores; the four demo firms and the other 41 stores pass. No repair is safe without inventing documents, so they stay as known legacy rows; rebuilding those fixture stores clears them. |
| D-DLG-7 | 2026-09-30 | Reported: an audit entry's detail and the opening-stock draft can overflow at 1366 x 768. | Not a defect. Driven at 1366 x 768: one audit entry with 40 changed fields and 400-character values in phase 2's window, and an opening-stock draft with 13 lines. Neither overflows -- `AlertDialog` bounds its content's height and both bodies already sit in a `SingleChildScrollView` (the audit table also in `Phase2WideTable`), so the dialog stays within the screen and scrolls. Kept as regression guards: `desktop/test/dialog_overflow_test.dart` (3). |
| D-STK-12 | 2026-09-19 | A manual adjustment can take stock below zero (driven: 3 adjusted by -100 read -97). | Decided by Claude, industry standard: kept. Negative stock through an adjustment is a deliberate, tested behaviour here (`test_adjustment_updates_projection_and_negative_stock_summary`) and is reported on the stock summary, as Tally and Odoo allow negative stock by default rather than blocking the correction. |
| D-FIN-1 | 2026-09-19 | A customer who owed money could be deleted, leaving 1100 holding the balance (driven: a customer with a 500.00 advance deleted, 204). Decided by Claude, industry standard: a customer or supplier who owes, holds an advance, or has an open or draft document cannot be deleted (set them inactive instead); delete no longer reverses the opening journal and restore re-posts one a pre-fix delete reversed. TEST01's five customers deleted on 2026-09-16 still carry 1,960.00 -- restoring them brings 1100 back into agreement; left for the owner. | #486 | `test_a_customer_who_owes_money_cannot_be_deleted`, `test_restoring_a_customer_whose_delete_reversed_its_balance_reposts_it`, `test_a_vendor_with_an_open_bill_cannot_be_deleted` |
| D-FIN-2 | 2026-09-19 | A document's journal could be reversed by hand while the document stood (driven: a receipt's journal reversed, the receipt then could not be). The hand-reverse endpoint refuses any journal with a `source_module` -- cancel or return the document instead; the desktop enables Reverse only for hand journals; the invoice-cancel lookup matches `reversal_of_id IS NULL`. | #488 | `test_a_documents_journal_cannot_be_reversed_by_hand`; `journal_entry_test.dart` |
| D-FIN-3 | 2026-09-19 | A locked financial year stopped nothing through the API (driven: a period of a locked year closed, reopened and posted into). A locked year takes no postings and its periods cannot be added, closed, reopened or edited; its audit rows record the lock. Decided by Claude: the lock stays irreversible, by design. | #490 | `test_a_locked_year_takes_no_postings_and_its_periods_stay_as_they_are` |
| D-FIN-4 | 2026-09-19 | The receivable endpoint moved INVOICE, TCS, LOYALTY, ADVANCE_APPLY and REFUND with no journal (driven: four types moved a balance, journal count unchanged). Each is refused and pointed at the endpoint that records it; CREDIT_NOTE, which posts a journal, stays. | #492 | `test_the_receivable_endpoint_refuses_what_another_module_records` |
| D-FIN-5 | 2026-09-19 | A reversal dated today in UTC could land the day before what it undid. A reversal is dated the later of today and the original's date; a typed date before the original is refused; an invoice cancel dates its receivable credit with the reversal. | #493 | `test_a_bill_cancelled_before_utc_catches_up_is_not_undone_the_day_before`, `test_a_reversal_cannot_be_asked_for_before_its_original` |
| D-FIN-6 | 2026-09-19 | Periods could overlap and be re-dated from under their journals, and posting picked an unordered period (driven: an overlapping period accepted; a period holding journals re-dated). Overlaps are refused; a period with journals keeps its dates; a year cannot move past its periods; the covering period is chosen by one fixed order. | #495 | `test_periods_do_not_overlap_and_keep_the_dates_their_journals_carry`, `test_posting_picks_one_period_the_same_way_every_time` |
| D-FIN-7 | 2026-09-19 | The balance sheet dropped CONTROL-type accounts (driven: 50.00 on a CONTROL TCS account left assets 50.00, liabilities 0). A CONTROL account goes where its mapped purpose puts it; unmapped or two-sided ones go by the sign of their balance. | #497 | `test_the_balance_sheet_carries_control_accounts_where_their_purpose_puts_them` |
| D-FIN-8 | 2026-09-19 | A mapped control account could be deactivated, and an inactive one mapped (driven: 1100 deactivated). Both refused, naming the purposes. | #498 | `test_a_mapped_account_stays_active_and_an_inactive_one_is_not_mapped` |
| D-FIN-9 | 2026-09-19 | A hand journal could take a document's reference, and the rolled-back number was re-issued on every retry (driven: three receipts refused 409 with the same number). Decided by Claude: hand journals keep to their own `JV-` space, which no numbering rule may use; a settlement steps over a number already held. | #500 | `test_a_hand_journal_cannot_take_a_documents_reference` and two more |
| D-FIN-10 | 2026-09-19 | Ageing ignored points spent on a bill, and `as_of` only moved the day count (driven: Record Receipt 383.21, ageing 483.21). One `settled_against()` serves both; `as_of` ages what was owed that day. | #501 | `test_the_ageing_ages_what_record_receipt_says_is_owed`, `test_as_of_ages_what_was_owed_on_that_day` |
| D-FIN-11 | 2026-09-19 | Hand journals could post to 1100, 1200 and 2300 (driven: all three POSTED). Decided by Claude, industry standard (reconcile-only accounts): receivable, payable, inventory, GRNI, commission and loyalty payable and every CONTROL account are left to their documents; tax, TCS, cash, bank, income, expense and equity stay open to hand journals. | #504 | `test_a_hand_journal_leaves_the_sub_ledger_accounts_to_their_documents` |

Decided by Claude, industry standard (three-way match; nothing used direct billing): the flag is gone and a purchase-order source is refused on the header and on every line. | #499 | `test_a_bill_cannot_skip_the_receipt`, `test_a_return_cannot_skip_the_receipt` |

| D-SELL-32 | 2026-09-19 | A quotation ignored an offer's bill discount, free gift and free shipping (driven: quote 5,678.16, the same order 5,265.16). Invoice, order, quotation, note and return all use it; an unknown state keeps the old behaviour. Purchase side: D-CMP-14. | #507 | 10 tests across 9 files; a guard stops a module naming its own type |
| D-CMP-2 | 2026-09-19 | Sales returns never reached GSTR-1 or 3B. A completed return is a credit note (CGST Act s.34): CDNR, a new CDNUR for an unregistered buyer on a B2CL bill, else netted off B2CS; 3B deducts it, cess included. | #508 | 4 backend tests; 6 desktop |
| D-CMP-3 | 2026-09-19 | Editing an active tax rule left the old version ACTIVE and matching. An edit retires or end-dates the old version in the same transaction; also fixed: editing a DRAFT rule with a condition answered 409. Stores already holding two ACTIVE versions are not repaired (SQL in the PR). | #509 | 4 tests |
| D-CMP-4 | 2026-09-19 | Returns and e-invoice rounded CGST and SGST separately (driven: journal 73.71, GSTR-1 36.86 + 36.86). `settle_to_ledger` makes the components add to what the journal credited. | #510 | 3 tests |
| D-CMP-5 | 2026-09-19 | A registration could be withdrawn, and the bill cancelled, under a live e-way bill. Both refused, naming it (NIC rule). | #511 | 2 tests |
| D-CMP-6 | 2026-09-19 | Re-registering a withdrawn invoice reissued the same IRN. Refused (NIC: a cancelled IRN is not reused); the withdrawn row stays as history. | #513 | backend + widget test |
| D-CMP-7 | 2026-09-19 | TCS on a back-dated receipt counted later receipts. The running total counts only receipts dated on or before it, within its financial year. | #514 | 1 test |
| D-CMP-8 | 2026-09-19 | Tax imports and the GST template committed row by row. All-or-nothing. | #516 | 4 tests |
| D-CMP-9 | 2026-09-19 | Tax deletes, restores, bulk actions and settings wrote no audit row, and a profile a rule uses could be deleted. All audited; that delete refused, naming the rules; reading settings writes nothing. | #519 | 5 tests |
| D-CMP-10 | 2026-09-19 | B2CL threshold, nil/exempt and DOCS were off. B2CL is Rs 1,00,000 from 2024-08-01 (Notification 12/2024-CT), 2,50,000 before; nil/exempt/non-GST go to Table 8 and 3B 3.1(c)/(e); DOCS shows cancelled. Decided by Claude, industry standard: statute as cited -- Notification 12/2024-Central Tax (10 July 2024) sets the B2CL limit at Rs 1,00,000 from 1 August 2024. | #520 | 4 backend tests; widget test |
| D-CMP-11 | 2026-09-19 | A filed month changed when a bill in it was cancelled later. Decided by Claude: with no record of filing, the GSTR-1 due date (11th of the next month) stands in; a later cancellation stays in its month and is declared in the month it happened. | #522 | 2 tests |
| D-CMP-12 | 2026-09-19 | TCS was collected under s.206C(1H), omitted by the Finance Act 2025 from 2025-04-01. Receipts from that date are not applicable; earlier ones untouched. Decided by Claude, industry standard: statute as cited -- the Finance Act 2025 omitted s.206C(1H) of the Income-tax Act with effect from 1 April 2025. | #524 | 1 test |
| D-BUY-15 | 2026-09-19 | A purchase bill could lift its own quantity cap with `allow_over_invoice` (driven: 60 billed against a receipt of 6, approved 7,080.00). Decided by Claude, industry standard: no tolerance; the fields are gone. | #515 | revert fails |
| D-BUY-16 | 2026-09-19 | A goods receipt could lift the ordered-quantity cap (driven: 20 more received against an order already full). Fields gone; capped at ordered less received; received now counts COMPLETED and CLOSED receipts (closing one had un-received it). Firm-level tolerance is a backlog item. | #517 | 2 tests |
| D-BUY-17 | 2026-09-19 | A bill or return line was looked up by id alone (driven: a bill for 10 units never received, a return taking stock to -10, a return on a cancelled bill).
